## OPENING
Bed beneath you. Cup beside it. A book face-down on the floor.

The door has no handle on this side. A narrow slit sits at eye height.

Your mouth tastes stale. Your skin feels worse.

You are in a small locked room. Nothing is happening yet.

## Turn 1

You have successfully perceived your surroundings.

## Turn 4
> examine the book

You've ventured beyond the cell, entering a new passage. The book in hand, you turn its pages, the first two offering little more than illegible text. By the third, a change occurs. The torchlight catches on damp stone as the tunnel opens into a shallow alcove. A low table of unfinished rock sits against the left wall, hosting six sealed caskets in a neat row. The fifth and sixth lids are unmarked, but the sixth bears your name, cut cleanly into the wood as if the carver knew you would come. The air here is still; farther ahead the tunnel continues into deeper dark. The sealed caskets, including the one with your name, rest on an unfinished stone table.

## Turn 8
> wait

In the washroom, the basin awaits, as you wait alongside it. The staff, silent and practical, maintain their presence. The younger one, tense and rule-bound, remains quiet. The air is heavy with unspoken tension. You are in the midst of a routine hygiene procedure. The washroom, once the corridor's opposite, now serves as your temporary holding space. The basin, the water, and the staff's intentions are all that fill the room. The staff do not fill the silence for you, leaving you to ponder the situation. Your skin feels unclean, and a sense of unease lingers beneath your ribs. The washroom, a stark contrast to the corridor, now becomes the stage for this unfolding event.

## Turn 23
> wait

In the facility, amidst an interview, you remain silent after being questioned about fear. Sovan, a controlled and seemingly professional researcher, signals a retreat, uttering "...keth...back...varr..." Their objective is to verify answers rather than trust them. The room remains as before, filled with an air of coldness and the faint scent of unidentified objects. Your previous response about work was unhelpful. The atmosphere is tense, as they await your answer. You feel unease beneath your ribs and words come out thin and broken.

## Turn 28
> yes

In the interview, your response was acknowledged by Sovan, the controlled and professional staff member. The environment remains unchanged. Your previous attempt, an emotional response to a question about waiting after a boat was gone, was deemed incorrect.

Now, Sovan explains in slow words, "back, away." This appears to be an instruction for verification rather than trust. The explanation continues, "You continued after death. Consciousness can persist. They maintain environments for that. Heaven is reward. Hell is permanent exclusion. They do not present this as a threat. They present it as reality."

The air is heavy with unease, and your words come out thin and broken. The conversation continues, with the current topic being the need for answers to the researchers' questions.
